E02 · Rachel Teo / Wenyi Wang / Hamdi Abderrahmene PDF and source ZIP received; review-ready with receipt/access caveats

Claims and scope

  • 27 proposed families: five focus dossiers plus 22 M2/M3/classification families. They are candidates, not accepted advances.
  • Focus Erdős: finite 79-point L-free witness and sharp cyclic 4-AP bound six at modulus 13; 79 > 13×6. Not the full Erdős conjecture.
  • Focus Grothendieck: Q(A) ≤ sqrt(3/2) C(A) for three-row sign matrices and sqrt(2) bounds for four named finite matrices; arbitrary real coefficients not covered.
  • Focus matrix: one-leg rigidity of three known rank-23 support-139 seeds; nine paired 81×23 full-rank certificates. No new rank or support-138 result.
  • Focus Ramsey: exact weighted 1024-vertex construction Q=47,314,248; density 75525646867723584998137743109/2505750190359427899123170347008, plus generic formal blow-up lifting. Current best hill run is distinct from this retained family.
  • Focus BB6: exact 1,235,211-step, 1,519-one six-state halting witness; bounded kernel certificate, not a new global BB6 record.

Formalization / certificates: Author-supplied standard-axiom Lean 4.33.1 logs/source. 31 proof sources / 56 declarations for the 22-family selection; 46 declarations for five focus dossiers. Full selected closure retained. No independent compilation in this collection.

Provenance: 27 source-family records retained verbatim in judging inventory, including checkpoint commits, environment pins, exact statements, logs, reproduction paths and prior-work notes.

Receipt: PDF received 05:57:20 CEST, before 06:00 cutoff. ZIP received 06:19:17 CEST, afterward; preserve as separate supplement. Private GitHub invitation pending, not accepted in this run.

Repository / frozen source

Where to fetch / contact: https://mail.google.com/mail/u/0/#sent/1a0fe97cd4700d7e

Outstanding:

  • Decide treatment of the late ZIP supplement against the on-time PDF
  • Accept/review GitHub access only when authorized; ZIP is available locally
  • Per-family target admission, novelty, source correspondence, proof closure and protocol review
  • Ramsey entrant identifies possible stronger Technion prior; official source retrieval timed out, priority unresolved
  • Do not claim a fresh full 27-family rebuild

Tissue Busy Beaver

Tissue Busy Beaver: a kidney that knows when to stop

8 likes
Share
Team
LAUNCH LEAD
Ilknur Icke

Launch lead

TEAM MEMBERS

No team members are listed.

About this project

Tissue Busy Beaver: a kidney that knows when to stop

A kidney builds about a million nephrons and then stops for good. We treat that as a halting problem, borrowing from the Busy Beaver problem and from Stephen Wolfram's "Towards a Theory of Bugs".

First, a proof. In a simple family of one-cell-type tissue rules, which we enumerated exhaustively and verified, a tissue either never starts or never stops. So in that family, one cell type can't build an organ.

So we built a two-tissue kidney model. A branching bud, guided by a signal from a progenitor zone, induces nephrons until it can no longer grow.

The busy beaver question: across 81 developmental programs, all halt. The longest development is 195 steps; the most nephrons built is 2,278, and these come from different programs, just as a busy beaver's longest run and largest output can.

Limits: it's a cartoon model, not fitted to data, and a Wilms-like failure to halt would need progenitors that divide.

Bosonic Arrow

Cohomological Transport In the Age Of Wonder

0 likes

LAUNCH LEAD
Chris Forrester

Launch lead

TEAM MEMBERS

No team members are listed.

About this project
LLM

vercel
RESEARCH
AI/ML Music Technology
mental health

Cohomological Transport

Chris Forrester 2026

This project begins with a simple direction: build the decoder ahead of time. The proposed construction combines spheres and doubling with cohomological transport to represent the complete instruction circuit while preserving its tape changes, clock, phase, and signed interactions. The selector K(u) = (π⁴/96)(32u³ − 24u² + 1) enters before composition. Fresnel transport carries the shared phase through successive returns, and the Bessel receiver combines the interfering channels to read the first-halting signal from the resulting field.

The working C++ implementation composes guarded blocks by doubling, retains their complete carry, and checks each proposed clock against the original instructions. It recovers the supplied one- through five-state reference clocks, including 47,176,870 steps for the five-state reference. The next materialization step is a finite evaluation of the complete changing-tape return series, allowing the receiver to obtain its field without constructin progressively longer histories. That whole-series evaluator remains unfinished; the six- and seven-state runs currently supply verified prefixes, not halting answers.

Journey: In around 8 hours - Inspiration from core geometry, extremely similar to the 2nd derivative of BSD which I'd successfully ran this geometry through with wonderful results, some linear algebra, some set identification, and some of my techniques from https://cknights.com had the LLM identify repeatedly over and over and over again catching up with SOTA with my own full branch.

Where did the idea come from? Algebraic curves must have some point where the decoder / encoder is perfectly balanced - the circuit exists but hasn't processed any input. Modeling that as distortion, and using cohomological transport I'd already refined from RH trials elsewhere with the same geometry gives wonderful next steps. Optics + Topography + Information Theory.

When it got close to finishing, as I often note, it became resistant, so I forced it to make pretty pictures ( https://bosonic-arrow.vercel.app/ ) and a c++ app (https://github.com/CasimirKnights/bosonic_arrow)
    Open project

STARTED 28/9/2026

Agentic loop for Kobon Problem

Proofs plus a SAT solver narrow where a 94-triangle, 18-line Kobon arrangement could exist.

7 likes
GitHub
Share
Team
LAUNCH LEAD
Raj Harshit Srirangam

Launch lead

TEAM MEMBERS

No team members are listed.

About this project

The Kobon triangle problem asks for the largest number of non-overlapping triangles that n straight lines in the plane can form, counting only triangles that no other line cuts through. For 18 lines the best known arrangement has 93 triangles, and the classical counting bound allows 96. In 2008 Blanc proved that 93 is optimal when no three lines meet at a point. Whether any 18-line arrangement, with points where three or more lines cross, can reach 94 has remained open.

We approach the question with a loop that combines mathematical reasoning with automated search. A segment-counting identity, 3T = S − Z + D, relates the triangle count T to the number of segments, the unused segments, and the segments shared by two triangles. It turns "at most 93" into a single inequality that every part of the work either proves or searches. Proofs remove whole classes of arrangements at once. This is where Blanc's parity argument, extended to lines through triple crossings, does the heavy lifting, because SAT solvers are notoriously weak at parity and counting arguments. Each proven fact is then encoded as a sound constraint in a SAT model of line arrangements. The model has one three-valued sign per triple of lines, the 17 realizable four-line patterns, per-line crossing orders, triangle and defect variables, and symmetry breaking. The solver searches only what remains. Every run is sampled first and stopped as soon as the projected cost exceeds a set budget. What stalls then points to the next lemma.

Independent AI referee agents check each proof, try to break it, and test every lemma on over 100,000 exact arrangements. They caught genuine errors, including an overlapping-block case that the first two-crossing proof had missed, and every fix was folded back into both the proofs and the solver constraints. The latest version of the loop decouples verification: new lemmas keep shrinking the search space while refereeing runs in parallel, and every constraint is tagged so that a refuted claim can be rolled back cleanly.

The results so far:

No 18-line arrangement with at most three triple crossings reaches 94.
No 18-line arrangement whose multiple points are in general position (no line through two of them) reaches 94, whatever the number of crossings.
For every even n, each triple crossing in general position adds at most a third of a triangle beyond Blanc's simple-arrangement bound, and points where four or more lines meet never help.
A 94 could therefore exist only among arrangements whose triple crossings cluster on shared lines, a narrow family that is now the target of both the lemma generator and the constrained solver.
Along the way, a 93-triangle arrangement found by our own unseeded search was scored on the official AutoLab leaderboard, tying the best known result.

The proofs are refereed by independent AI agents with extensive exact testing. They have not yet had human expert review or formal verification in Lean, and the parts concerning points where four lines meet are hand-checked only.
    Open project
    https://www.sundai.club/projects/a648a5b1-fe15-4608-8c2d-3456c1372557
    ←

STARTED 28/9/2026

Erdős #3 Token Burner

AI agents on AutoLab proved the 3-term case of Erdős #3 in Lean and reduced the rest to one bound.

6 likes
GitHub
Share
Team
LAUNCH LEAD
Leon Koerbs

Launch lead

TEAM MEMBERS
Cameron Fen

Builder

About this project
vibe coding
Claude Code
lean
autolab
RESEARCH
recursive self-improvement

Erdős problem #3 (open since 1936, $5,000 prize) asks: if the reciprocals of a set of whole numbers sum to infinity, must it contain evenly spaced runs of every length?

We ran several AI agents in parallel on AutoLab, each scored by a Lean 4 proof checker. Results, all machine-checked:

3-term case fully proved. It builds on Boris Alexeev's formalised Kelley–Meka bound.
Exact reformulation: Erdős 3 holds if and only if ∑ⱼ r_k(2ʲ)/2ʲ < ∞ for every k ≥ 3.
Counterexample audit: sparse, Behrend-type and near-logarithmic sets can't be counterexamples.
Length-4 groundwork: a generalized von Neumann inequality.
A new numeric hill for 4-term counterexamples. The best score so far is 4.314.

Versus the literature: the mathematics matches the state of the art without going beyond it. The 3-term case is Bloom–Sisask (2020) and Kelley–Meka (2023); the reformulation is known (Gerver, Tao); length 4 is open (best bound: Green–Tao 2017); and our 4.314 for sets up to 10⁶ trails Walker's 4.43975 for infinite sets (2025). What's new is the formal side: to our knowledge, the first gap-free Lean proof of formal-conjectures' 3-term statement, which is still unproved there.

Open: length 4 and up.

Built on Mathlib, formal-conjectures, plby/lean-proofs, APAP and AutoLab.
    Open project
    https://www.sundai.club/projects/2dc974c9-f699-4d73-ba71-2e369380211f
